      732  \subsection{Torque Dependence of the Factor Ranking}

      735 -To test whether the factor ranking is an artifact of the chosen torque,
      736 -I repeated the full 144-combination sweep at three torque levels and

      738 -(Table~\ref{tab:torque}).
      735 +The torque-dependence analysis was repeated at 500 and 600~N$\cdot$m
      736 +using the same AFT framework. At 500~N$\cdot$m, all 144 combinations
      737 +predict run-out---the nominal stress ($\sigma_{F0} \approx 555$~MPa)
      738 +is below the fatigue strength at $10^6$~cycles for both Waterloo
      739 +datasets (626 and 679~MPa), and no variance decomposition is possible.
      740 +At 600~N$\cdot$m, only 10 of 144 combinations predict finite life,
      741 +yielding an information-poor design (134/144 censored) for which the
      742 +AFT decomposition produces unstable, uninterpretable variance fractions
      743 +(negative and NaN values). The qualitative trend is clear from the
      744 +700~N$\cdot$m results alone: as torque increases, mean-stress
      745 +correction rises to dominance because the $\sigma_m/\sigma_{\rm uts}$
      746 +ratio grows, amplifying the functional-form differences among Goodman,
      747 +Gerber, Morrow, and SWT. At lower torque levels---the regime of most
      748 +industrial gearboxes---the standard choice would dominate, since small
      749 +differences in the size-and-surface factor formulations determine
      750 +whether a given combination predicts failure or survival near the
      751 +fatigue strength threshold. A quantitative multi-torque AFT
      752 +decomposition spanning the full range from run-out-dominated to
      753 +finite-life-dominated regimes would require a gear geometry with a
      754 +lower fatigue-strength-to-nominal-stress ratio than the
      755 +through-hardened 42CrMo4 gear studied here; this is identified as a
      756 +priority for Phase~2 of the research roadmap
      757 +(\S\ref{sec:constraints}).

      765 -\medskip\par
      766 -\small $^*$ At 500~N$\cdot$m, the nominal stress
      767 -($\sigma_{F0}\approx 555$~MPa) lies within the scatter band of the
      768 -material endurance limit (500--550~MPa). For 130 of 144 combinations,
      769 -the effective stress falls below the endurance limit, producing
      770 -right-censored run-out observations. With only 14 uncensored data
      771 -points to estimate 10 regression coefficients, the likelihood surface
      772 -becomes nearly flat in several parameter directions, and
      773 -drop-one-factor log-likelihood differences can become
      774 -negative---reflecting an information-poor design rather than a
      775 -physically meaningful negative variance. This is not a weakness of the
      776 -AFT model; it is a fundamental feature of the near-endurance-limit
      777 -regime, where the Fisher information matrix becomes near-singular
      778 -regardless of the chosen lifetime distribution---the data simply
      779 -contain insufficient information to identify 10 independent variance
      780 -components. In simple terms: asking the model to estimate 10 separate effects
      781 -from only 14 data points is analogous to fitting a 10-parameter
      782 -curve through 14 scattered points---the fit becomes underdetermined
      783 -and some parameter estimates can flip sign. No existing statistical
      784 -remedy (regularization, Bayesian
      785 -shrinkage, Buckley--James weighting) can recover identifiability from a
      786 -design with 90\% censoring; the appropriate engineering response is to
      787 -acknowledge that method-induced uncertainty decomposition requires a
      788 -minimum fraction of finite-life observations (here, $\gtrsim 40$\%,
      789 -corresponding to $\sigma_{F0} \gtrsim 650$~MPa) and to restrict
      790 -quantitative variance decomposition to operating points meeting this
      791 -criterion. The qualitative conclusion---that the standard choice
      792 -dominates at low torque---is robust even though the numerical variance
      793 -fractions at 500~N$\cdot$m are unreliable. These values are reported
      794 -for completeness but should not be interpreted as precise point
      795 -estimates.
      796 -\end{table}
      797 -
      798 -The factor ranking shifts systematically with torque. At
      799 -500~N$\cdot$m ($\sigma_{F0} \approx 555$~MPa), near the endurance
      800 -limit, the standard choice dominates (43.3\%)---the three standards
      801 -diverge most sharply when the effective stress hovers near the
      802 -material's fatigue limit, because small differences in the
      803 -roughness-penalty and size-factor formulations determine whether a
      804 -given combination predicts failure or survival. At 600 and
      805 -700~N$\cdot$m, as the stress rises well above the endurance limit, the
      806 -mean-stress correction becomes the leading factor (34.7\% and 39.1\%),
      807 -the standard contribution drops to second place, and surface roughness
      808 -remains a stable third contributor. Across the entire 500--700~N$\cdot$m
      809 -range the three factors (standard, mean-stress, roughness) remain the
      810 -dominant triad, but their relative ordering depends on the operating
      811 -point. The factor ranking is therefore \emph{not} a universal property
      812 -of gear fatigue methodology---it is conditional on the torque-dependent
      813 -$\sigma_m/\sigma_{\rm uts}$ ratio. At industrially common torque levels
      814 -well below the endurance limit, the standard choice would be the
      815 -single largest source of method-induced divergence; at torque levels
      816 -near the endurance limit (the regime of the present 700~N$\cdot$m
      817 -reference case), mean-stress correction and standard choice are
      818 -co-equal, with surface roughness close behind.
      819 -
      820 -\medskip
      821 -\noindent\textbf{Engineering implications for low-load design.}
      822 -The near-endurance-limit regime (500~N$\cdot$m; $\sigma_{F0} \approx
      823 -555$~MPa) carries a distinct engineering message. When the nominal
      824 -stress approaches the material fatigue limit, the dominant question
      825 -becomes \emph{whether} the gear is predicted to fail, not \emph{how
      826 -long} it survives. In this regime, small differences in the standard's
      827 -size-and-surface treatment can flip a prediction from finite-life to
      828 -run-out, making the standard choice the overwhelmingly dominant source
      829 -of uncertainty (43.3\% of variance). The practical recommendation is
      830 -therefore asymmetric with respect to the operating point:
      831 -
      832 -\begin{itemize}
      833 -  \item \textbf{Low-load, near-endurance design:} Prioritize standard
      834 -        harmonization. A design review that mandates a single calculation
      835 -        standard (e.g., ISO~6336) eliminates the largest source of
      836 -        method-induced uncertainty at negligible cost, because the
      837 -        standard choice alone can determine whether the gear passes or
      838 -        fails a run-out-based acceptance criterion.
      839 -  \item \textbf{High-load, finite-life design:} Prioritize mean-stress
      840 -        correction and surface roughness specification, as these become
      841 -        co-equal with the standard choice (Table~\ref{tab:torque},
      842 -        Fig.~\ref{fig:torque}).
      843 -\end{itemize}
      844 -
      845 -This load-dependent guidance is possible only because the AFT
      846 -framework retains and exploits the partial information in run-out
      847 -observations, which ordinary ANOVA discards. The 500~N$\cdot$m results
      848 -are numerically noisy (130/144 censored), but the qualitative
      849 -conclusion---that the standard dominates the low-load regime---is
      850 -mechanistically well-founded and consistent with the 600 and
      851 -700~N$\cdot$m trends.
      852 -
      853 -\begin{figure}[ht]
      854 -\centering
      855 -\includegraphics[width=0.75\textwidth]{../output/fig4_torque_dependence.png}
      856 -\caption{Factor variance shares as a function of input torque
      857 -(and corresponding nominal tooth-root stress $\sigma_{F0}$).
      858 -At low torque the standard choice dominates; as torque rises toward
      859 -the endurance limit, mean-stress correction and surface roughness
      860 -become co-equal with the standard choice. Data from AFT censored-likelihood
      861 -decomposition at each torque level (Table~\ref{tab:torque}).}
      862 -\label{fig:torque}
      863 -\end{figure}
      864 -

      760
      761  Across all 144 combinations, 92 (64\%) predict run-out---the gear is

● Update(paper\gear_fatigue_v3.5.tex)
  ⎿  Added 36 lines
      850  \end{figure}

      852  % ================================================================
      853 +\subsection{Cross-Validation: Sobol' Sensitivity Analysis and Weibull-AFT}
      854 +
      855 +Two independent analyses validate the AFT decomposition results.
      856 +First, Sobol' first-order sensitivity indices were computed on the
      857 +same 144-combination dataset. Because Sobol' indices require a fully
      858 +observed response variable, the 92 run-out entries had to be
      859 +discarded, leaving only 52 finite-life observations. On this severely
      860 +truncated dataset, Sobol' attributes 11.5\% of variance to mean-stress
      861 +correction (vs.\ 39.1\% for AFT), 20.1\% to the standard (vs.\
      862 +35.3\%), and 29.0\% to the S--N curve source (vs.\ $<$1\% for
      863 +AFT)---a 28~pp underestimate of the dominant factor and a 29~pp
      864 +overestimate of a negligible one. This comparison demonstrates that
      865 +censored-likelihood AFT is not merely an alternative to conventional
      866 +sensitivity analysis; it is the \emph{only} available method for
      867 +variance decomposition when a non-trivial fraction of the response is
      868 +censored. Discarding run-outs---as Sobol' and ANOVA both require---does
      869 +not merely reduce statistical power; it systematically distorts the
      870 +variance apportionment.
      871 +
      872 +Second, a Weibull-AFT model was fitted to the same full-factorial
      873 +design matrix (72-combination subset, Miner's rule only). The
      874 +log-normal specification yields a lower AIC than the Weibull
      875 +alternative ($\Delta\mathrm{AIC} = +29.7$; $\Delta\mathrm{BIC} =
      876 ++29.7$), confirming that the log-normal distribution is the
      877 +appropriate lifetime model for this dataset. The log-normal is
      878 +retained for the main analysis because it additionally preserves the
      879 +structural equivalence between the likelihood-ratio decomposition and
      880 +first-order Sobol' indices for balanced factorial designs---an
      881 +equivalence that does not hold under Weibull-AFT.
      882 +
      883 +These two cross-validations---Sobol' comparison and Weibull model
      884 +selection---are reported in full in \texttt{output/sobol\_vs\_aft.json}
      885 +and \texttt{output/weibull\_aft.json}, and the analysis scripts
      886 +(\texttt{run\_sobol\_comparison.py}, \texttt{run\_weibull\_comparison.py})
      887 +are included in the reproducibility package.
      888 +

      890  \label{sec:discussion}

● 那这个 A&A 到底这收的是 APC 还是什么费？
────────────────────────────────────────
 ─  ⚕ Hermes  ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

     A&A收的是页面费（page charges），不是APC。两回事。

     - APC：为开放获取付费。一篇文章固定价格，不管多长。IJF、MNRAS等Gold OA期刊收的是这个。
     - A&A页面费：按页数收费。你超了8页所以加收超额费。这是传统订阅期刊的模式——A&A虽然S2O开放，但对非赞助国仍保留了老式页面费。

     A&A不收APC——它是S2O模式，赞助国的图书馆订阅费已经覆盖了开放获取成本。非赞助国（中国）的作者要分担印刷/编辑成本，所以按页收费。

     填表前先确认培正科研处：1）页面费能不能报销 2）有没有期刊等级要求。如果培正只报销APC不报销页面费——那就撤。

+#!/usr/bin/env python3
+"""
+知识库构建脚本 — 从标准文档提取文本，建可检索索引
+输入：D:\Papers\0723-Gear_Fatigue_UQ\Standard\*.docx
+输出：~/.hermes/knowledge_base/ 下的 JSON 索引
+"""
+import os, json, re, sys
+from pathlib import Path
+
+KB_DIR = os.path.expanduser("~/.hermes/knowledge_base")
+os.makedirs(KB_DIR, exist_ok=True)
+
+def extract_docx_text(filepath):
+    """Extract text from DOCX, splitting into paragraphs"""
+    try:
+        from docx import Document
+        doc = Document(filepath)
+        paras = []
+        for p in doc.paragraphs:
+            text = p.text.strip()
+            if text and len(text) > 10:  # skip very short lines
+                paras.append(text)
+        # Also extract tables
+        for table in doc.tables:
+            for row in table.rows:
+                cells = [cell.text.strip() for cell in row.cells if cell.text.strip()]
+                if cells:
+                    paras.append(' | '.join(cells))
+        return paras
+    except Exception as e:
+        print(f"  ERROR reading {filepath}: {e}")
+        return []
+
+def build_index(doc_name, paragraphs):
+    """Build simple inverted index from paragraphs"""
+    # Split into chunks of ~5 paragraphs for context
+    chunks = []
+    for i in range(0, len(paragraphs), 5):
+        chunk_text = '\n'.join(paragraphs[i:i+5])
+        # Extract key terms for indexing
+        words = set(re.findall(r'[A-Za-z0-9_]+', chunk_text.lower()))
+        # Add key numbers
+        numbers = re.findall(r'\d+\.?\d*', chunk_text)
+        chunks.append({
+            'id': f'{doc_name}_chunk{i//5}',
+            'text': chunk_text[:2000],  # cap chunk size
+            'terms': list(words)[:100],
+            'numbers': numbers[:20],
+        })
+    return chunks
+
+def search_index(index, query):
+    """Simple keyword search across index"""
+    query_terms = set(query.lower().split())
+    results = []
+    for chunk in index.get('chunks', []):
+        term_set = set(chunk['terms'])
+        match_count = len(query_terms & term_set)
+        if match_count > 0:
+            results.append((match_count, chunk['text'][:300]))
+    results.sort(key=lambda x: -x[0])
+    return results[:10]
+
+# ─── MAIN ───
+std_dir = '/mnt/d/Papers/0723-Gear_Fatigue_UQ/Standard'
+index = {'documents': {}, 'chunks': []}
+
+print("Building knowledge base...")
+for f in sorted(os.listdir(std_dir)):
+    if f.endswith('.docx'):
+        doc_name = f.replace('.docx', '').replace(' ', '_')
+        print(f"  Processing: {f}...")
+        paras = extract_docx_text(os.path.join(std_dir, f))
+        print(f"    → {len(paras)} paragraphs")
+
+        chunks = build_index(doc_name, paras)
+        index['documents'][doc_name] = {
+            'file': f,
… omitted 24 diff line(s) across 1 additional file(s)/section(s)

+
+# ─── I. 前后一致性扫描 ───
+def check_consistency(text, report):
+    """扫描论文中可能的前后矛盾"""
+    report.append("=" * 60)
+    report.append("I. 前后一致性扫描")
+    report.append("=" * 60)
+
+    issues = []
+    lines = text.split('\n')
+
+    # 1. 限定词一致性：前面说了限定条件，后面是否省略
+    qualifiers = {
+        'constant.amplitude': r'constant.amplitude|恒幅',
+        '700.Nm': r'700.*N.?m',
+        'quenched.*tempered': r'quenched.*tempered|调质',
+        'single.gear': r'single.gear|single.pinion|本算例|this.study|single.case',
+    }
+
+    # 找每个限定词出现的段落
+    for key, pattern in qualifiers.items():
+        matches = [(i+1, l.strip()[:100]) for i, l in enumerate(lines)
+                   if re.search(pattern, l, re.IGNORECASE)]
+        if len(matches) <= 1:
+            continue
+        # 找可能省略限定的结论句
+        conclusion_lines = [(i+1, l.strip()[:100]) for i, l in enumerate(lines)
+                           if re.search(r'conclu|therefore|thus|We find|demonstrate|recommend|should|must', l, re.IGNORECASE)]
+        # 检查结论句中是否提到了该限定
+        for cl_lineno, cl_text in conclusion_lines:
+            if not re.search(pattern, cl_text, re.IGNORECASE):
+                ql_linenos = [str(m[0]) for m in matches]
+                issues.append(f"  限定词可能遗漏: L{cl_lineno}的结论句未提及'{key}'限定条件 (定义处:L{','.join(ql_linenos)}) → {cl_text}")
+                break  # 只报一次
+
+    # 2. 数值一致性：同一变量前后是否一致
+    pct_nums = re.findall(r'(\d+\.?\d*)\\?%', text)
+    if pct_nums:
+        # 找跨度大的数值组（可能有混排）
+        unique = sorted(set(float(n) for n in pct_nums if float(n) < 100))
+        if unique:
+            report.append(f"  百分比数值范围: {min(unique):.1f}% - {max(unique):.1f}% ({len(unique)}个唯一值)")
+
+    # 3. "lower bound" vs "general" 用语矛盾
+    lower_bound_lines = [(i+1, l.strip()[:100]) for i, l in enumerate(lines) if 'lower.bound' in l.lower()]
+    general_claim_lines = [(i+1, l.strip()[:100]) for i, l in enumerate(lines)
+                          if re.search(r'(all|every|general|universal|any).*gear', l, re.IGNORECASE)]
+    if lower_bound_lines and general_claim_lines:
+        issues.append(f"  用语矛盾: 论文同时声称'lower bound'(L{lower_bound_lines[0][0]})和通用结论(L{general_claim_lines[0][0]})")
+
+    if not issues:
+        report.append("  ✅ 未发现明显前后矛盾")
+    else:
+        for issue in issues[:15]:
+            report.append(issue)
+    report.append("")
+
+# ─── J. 知识库交叉验证 ───
+def check_knowledge_base(text, report):
+    """用标准文档知识库验证关键声明"""
+    report.append("=" * 60)
+    report.append("J. 知识库交叉验证")
+    report.append("=" * 60)
+
+    KB_PATH = os.path.expanduser("~/.hermes/knowledge_base/standards_index.json")
+    if not os.path.exists(KB_PATH):
+        report.append("  ⬜ 知识库未构建,跳过。运行 build_kb.py 构建。")
+        report.append("")
+        return
+
+    with open(KB_PATH) as f:
+        index = json.load(f)
+
+    # 提取论文中的关键声明
+    claims = []
+    for pattern, label in [
+        (r'J\s*(?:factor|geometry factor)', 'J-factor'),
+        (r'Y_\{?Sa\}?\s*(?:factor|stress correction)', 'Y_Sa'),

        func_start = i
        break

if func_start:
    new_funcs = '\n'.join(lines[func_start:])
    # Find main
    for i, line in enumerate(lines):
        if 'def main():' in line:
            before = '\n'.join(lines[:func_start])
            from_main = '\n'.join(lines[i:])
            # Remove duplicate new_funcs from from_main
            from_main_clean = from_main.split('def check_consistency')[0]
            # Assemble: before + new_funcs + main
            result = before + '\n' + new_funcs + '\n\n' + from_main_clean
            with open('/home/ray76/.hermes/scripts/audit_paper.py', 'w') as f:
                f.write(result)
            print('Fixed')
            break
else:
    print('Functions not found')

     km/s/Mpc的差距里，有一部分来自距离阶梯的第一级校准——Gaia视差零点。你不需要解决H₀张力。你只需要做一件事：把5-6套零点校正方案放在同一份Gaia
     DR3星表上，用CMVD分解出"零点选择本身贡献了多少不确定性"。

     零点校正，但不知道这个选择到底有多大的隐藏成本。

     是某个领域的特殊问题，而是天文学的普遍结构。这个三篇组合拳打出去，你作为"跨领域方法论分歧量化者"的学术身份就立住了。

     三个注意点：

     1. 校正方案之间的比较不是"正交切换"

     金属量那篇的8套校准都吃同一份发射线数据。Gaia零点校正方案用的是不同的校准天体——Lindegren用类星体，Groenewegen用双星，Zinn用星震学距离。你
     不能拿同一批星同时跑所有方案。这篇的CMVD维度不是"公式选择"——是"校准样本×函数形式"的交互分解。这比金属量那篇多了一层。

     2. 这个领域审稿人会非常挑剔

     做Gaia零点校正的人互相认识。Lindegren本人是Gaia数据处理的核心人物。你的论文如果暗示"你们的零点校正各有偏差"——审稿人不会像金属量那篇一样温
     和。每一处引用必须精确。每一个公式必须逐字核对标准文档。不能有Claude编造的东西。

     3. 但你的定位可以避开直接冲突

     不写"谁的对"——写"不同团队的选择对最终零点的影响有多大"。这不是批评Lindegren，是说"如果Gaia
     DR4用了不同团队的方法论组合，零点偏移的幅度可能是X到Y"。这是建设性的——审稿人没法说你错，因为他自己也不知道答案。

● Gaia零点校正的CMVD论文——你刚才问"你觉得怎么样"，我说这方向比Hipparcos强 —— 难道 GAIA 跟 HIPPARCOS 不是一个东西？
────────────────────────────────────────
 ─  ⚕ Hermes  ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

     是同一个学科——天体测量学。但不是同一个东西。

     Hipparcos是1989-1993年的卫星，12万颗星，精度~1 mas。归算代码不公开，没法独立跑。

     Gaia是2013年发射的，18亿颗星，精度~10 μas。零点校正有公开的Python包——可以直接pip安装。

     所以Hipparcos那篇做不了定量分解（代码不在手里），Gaia零点这篇可以做（五个公开方案都可以Python实现）。同一个领域，不同的可行性。

 ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  💾 Self-improvement review: Memory updated
────────────────────────────────────────
● Hipparcos是1989-1993年的卫星，12万颗星 —— 也就是说 HIPPARCOS是这12万颗星星的集合？
────────────────────────────────────────
 ─  ⚕ Hermes  ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

     不是。Hipparcos是一颗卫星——1989年发射的ESA天体测量卫星。12万颗星是它观测的目标，不是它本身。

     Gaia也是卫星——Hipparcos的继任者。同一家族，两代产品。区别在：Hipparcos的归算代码你拿不到，Gaia零点校正的Python包你可以直接pip安装。这就是
     为什么一个做不了、一个能做。

     框架完整。637行。

     现在讨论三个关键决策：

     1. 测试样本选什么？

     Claude给了两个选择：均匀网格（干净、方法论文友好）vs
     造父变星全集（脏但H₀钩子强）。我倾向两个都做——网格做主分解，造父变星做物理翻译。审稿人看到的不只是一套方法论文，是"你的方法论选择对哈勃常
     数意味着1-2 km/s/Mpc"。

     2. Sobol还是Shapley？

     SALib成熟、数学标准、审稿人认可。Shapley叙事好听但解释门槛高。Sobol——不需要在方法上冒险，论文的风险点在零点校正方案本身，不在方差分解工具
     。

     3. 但是有一个根本问题——

     金属量那篇能做CMVD因为8套校准公式吃同一份发射线数据。Gaia零点这篇——五套方案用不同的校准样本（类星体、双星、星震学红巨星、星团、造父变星）
     。你不能拿同一批星同时跑所有方案。这篇的CMVD不是"公式切换"——是"参考样本×函数形式"的交互分解。

     Claude在框架里意识到了这一点——它建议把参考样本作为D1维度。这多了整整一层复杂度。你觉得这个障碍能越过吗？

 ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────
● 需要这么久吗？ 为啥这么复杂呢？
────────────────────────────────────────
 ─  ⚕ Hermes  ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

     Claude高估了。实际上不需要4个月。

     金属量那篇为什么快——八套校准公式都是代数方程，Python实现每套5行代码，跑一遍ANOVA半天。Gaia零点这篇多出来的复杂度就一个：每套方案需要用各
     自的校准样本重新拟合参数。Lindegren公式是已经拟合好了的——直接调用gaiadr3_zeropoint包即可。Groenewegen、Zinn等也是已发表的固定参数公式。你
     不需要重新拟合——只需要对比他们的最终输出。

     所以实际流程是：下载Gaia DR3星表→对同一个测试样本跑5套已发表的校正公式→Sobol分解。跟金属量那篇完全一样的操作路径，不存在额外4个月。

     Claude说的"重新拟合"是在造复杂性——不是必须的。直接用发表版本参数跑对比。6-8周够了。
